- P2 results: [pcc:unverified]

  - Crossover p*(g) (`P2/RESULTS_crossover.md`): about 270 at g=6, about 830 at g=7, above 10^4 at g=8. The g=7 and g=8 values are extrapolations.
- Sources checked: KB unavailable in this session (research-kb MCP not attached). Novelty statements say the method is classical (Enge-Gaudry).

### Decisions made (and why)

- **Patched the lab instrument rather than writing a new crate.** This keeps genus 2-5 count-identical to the lab baseline.
- **Required #J < 2^63 so every solve is certified.** As a result p is small at large genus, so nothing is claimed at cryptographic p.
- **Used expected-step rho as the primary reference.** A single executed walk varied 0.35-1.7x from its expectation.

## Multi-target 4-summand: tuned, frozen, fresh-curve holdout passed (`[pcc:unverified]`)

- New `examples/prime_fast_j0_multi4.rs` (committed 6875b1f51e, correctness-checked at 24 bits first): logs of a small factor base from one collection, each target decomposed into four signed orbit elements through the triple-sum table; every target verified by kG=Q and x==k.
- Tuned at 32 bits on separate curves (CSKIP=150, 6 rows per cell, baseline `ic_multi_solve_ex` c=0.5 in-row): best B4 = 44 at T=64 (46.9 ms vs baseline 198.7 ms) and 52 at T=512 (127.3 ms vs 359.3 ms). Rows in `multi4_tune/`. Setup (the B^3 table build) grows from 30 ms at B=28 to 318 ms at B=80, so the optimum B rises slowly with T.
- Froze (hash) `frozen_multi4_holdout.json` in a383db8868 before any holdout row: prediction base/sym4 >= 2.5 (T=64) and >= 1.8 (T=512) against the better of baseline c=0.5/0.8; abandon < 1.3 or any unverified target.
- Holdout CSKIP=1700 (fresh curves), 8 rows per cell, all 16 sym4 rows and baseline arms verified (every target), `verify_pf_cert.py` passes 1024 + 8192 recovered scalars: T=64 baseline c=0.5 203.4 ms and c=0.8 255.6 ms vs sym4 47.8 / 63.2 ms (ratios 4.26 / 4.05); T=512 baseline c=0.5 382.9 ms and c=0.8 293.4 ms vs sym4 130.3 / 134.7 ms (ratios 2.94 / 2.18). Against the better baseline c: 4.26 (T=64) and 2.18 (T=512), both predictions met, no abandon rule triggered.
- Same-curve folded multi-target BSGS (`prime_fast_j0_bsgs_multi`, same CSKIP and bits): 24.7 ms (T=64) and 106.5 ms (T=512), all verified. sym4 is 1.93x and 1.22x slower than BSGS at T=64 and T=512, down from 3.4x to 6.5x for the 3-summand multi-target solver.

- The multi-target 4-summand solver beats the tuned library baseline by about 2.2x to 4.3x at 32 bits (wall clock, one host, no isolation, 8 rows per cell, no validator replay yet), and closes most of the gap to folded BSGS at 512 targets, but BSGS is still faster at both T. Per-target cost scales linearly in T (about 0.19 ms per target at B=52) against BSGS's square-root growth, so I do not expect it to win at larger T without a larger table; not tested.

- Validator replay of the multi-target holdout; T=4096; speeding up the B^3 table build (it uses a full point addition per pair-sum); AWS-dependent gate artifacts; the PR #4645 required checks. Goal (parity with rho / strongest comparators) unmet.

## verify_pf_cert.py multi-target hole closed (`[pcc:unverified]`)

- `tools/verify_pf_cert.py` multi-target branch now requires len(q)=len(sym_rec)=T, no null scalars, base_rec complete whenever the row has `base_ok` or a base_rec, scalars reduced (0<=x<n), and sym4_ok/sym_ok/base_ok counters equal to T. Real rows (multi4_holdout 4 files, multi4_tune 14 files) still pass: 0 failing rows. 10 of 10 mutations (unreduced, null one/all/whole, truncated, base null variants, zeroed counter, +1) are now rejected; before: 8 accepted.

- Strictness chosen to match the validator's independent checker, so the repo checker is no weaker than the validator's.

- Not re-run on cert_replay/sym4 single-target dirs in this sparse tree; CI will. Large JSONL to artifact store, f4_fp.rs decision, PR #4645 CI, table-build speed-up, AWS-blocked gate artifacts. Goal unmet.


## Table-build speed-up analysis (`[pcc:unverified]`, no code change)

- Read `build4` (examples/prime_fast_j0_multi4.rs): the triple-sum table already uses one `batch_inv` per (i,j,m,s1) over 3(B-j-1) denominators, sharing one inverse between the sum and difference x-coordinates; about 78 ns per table entry at B=44 (37 ms, ~477k entries). Holdout T=64 split (row 0): build 37.3 ms, collection 10.7 ms, per-target 10.6 ms, total 58.7 ms.
- Estimate (not measured): merging the six (m,s1) batches into one batch per (i,j) would save about 1.4 field-mult-equivalents per entry, about 5% of build, below the +-20% single-host noise.

- Not implemented: even removing the whole build (37 ms) leaves 21 ms vs BSGS 24.7 ms at T=64, so build is the entire gap there, but a 5% saving cannot close it and could not be resolved with this noise. A gain needs a structurally smaller table (fewer entries per unit of relation yield), not faster entries. Next discriminating experiment: partial table (store only entries whose canonical key hashes into a fraction f of the space, scan with matching filter), which cuts build by 1/f and collection by the same factor; predicted break-even at T=64 needs f<=0.5 with extra candidates per relation x2, i.e. no net gain unless the build, not the candidate loop, dominates. To be tested before any claim.

- Partial-table experiment; PR #4645 CI/f4_fp.rs decision; large JSONL to artifact store; AWS-blocked gate artifacts. Goal unmet (behind BSGS and rho).


## Partial-table lead closed by analysis (`[pcc:unverified]`, no run)

- Checked the partial-table idea against `build4`: an entry's canonical key is only known after the point sum is computed, which is the cost being saved. Skipping entries therefore cannot reduce build cost; restricting which triples (i,j,l) are built is the same as shrinking B, which the B4 tuning grid (B=20..80, `multi4_tune/`) already covers.

- Lead closed without an experiment: it is not a new design, only the already-tuned B trade-off. Recorded as an obstruction. No run, no claim.
- Remaining levers with a real chance at the T=64 gap to BSGS (build is ~64% of sym4 time): (a) a table keyed by a cheaper hash of a partial sum (e.g. precomputed pair sums joined with single points, so each triple costs a table probe instead of a point addition: meet-in-the-middle from the scan side, trading build for more scan probes); (b) amortise the build across curves sharing a factor base (not applicable: tables depend on the curve). Next experiment: (a), predicted to move cost from build to collection; needs a model before code.

- (a) model and prototype; PR #4645 CI, f4_fp.rs decision, large JSONL to artifact store, AWS-blocked gate artifacts. Goal unmet.


## Pair-table meet-in-the-middle model: no gain predicted (`[pcc:unverified]`, model only)

- Modelled lead (a). Current scheme: triple table (~6B^3 entries, one point addition each) probed by single-candidate scan (R +- phi^k f_h). A pair-sum table probed by pair candidates has table ~6B^2, so per-relation cost p/B^2 per candidate (the 3-summand cost), worse; a pair table probed by single points is the existing scheme with the roles swapped. For B relations: build B^3 + scan B*p/B^3 candidates, optimum B^5 ~ p, exactly the already-tuned p^(3/5) trade-off.

- Closed: it only re-labels the existing balance, no prototype. Remaining gap to folded BSGS at T=64 is therefore a constant-factor matter in the build (about 78 ns/entry) or needs a different structural idea (e.g. exploiting the +-/orbit structure to shrink entries per relation, or sharing one table across many R values, which the multi-target scan already does).
- No improvement over the strongest baseline (folded BSGS) exists; claims stay limited to the tuned library baseline.

- PR #4645 CI, f4_fp.rs decision, large JSONL to artifact store, AWS-blocked gate artifacts, structural idea beyond B^5~p. Goal unmet.

## binary_falldeg_20261118 — binary (GF(2^n)) Koblitz fall-degree study (preregister + pilot)

Worktree `/home/user/crypto-wt/binary-falldeg`, branch `wt/binary-falldeg`
(sparse checkout). Subagent working inside the parent's lane; did not edit
`docs/ACTIVE.md`.


- Surveyed existing binary machinery: `src/cryptanalysis/{binary_semaev.rs,
  gf2_elim.rs, koblitz_groebner.rs, ffd_harness.rs, pq_groebner_f2.rs}`,
  `koblitz_bench.rs`, examples `hky_par.rs`/`hky_window.rs`. Found the exact
  binary summation-polynomial construction already in the tree:
  `koblitz_groebner::sym_semaev_s3` +
  `koblitz_groebner::build_decomposition_system` (the Weil-descended Semaev
  chain), exercised by `examples/hky_par.rs` (dense HKY mutant closure for
  `d_F`) and the library `first_fall_degree`/`solving_degree`.
- Read prior art: `research/fall_degree_bounds_20261008/` (binary HKY `d_F`
  with random/subfield bases, Sage + native `hky`), the prime-field
  `research/prime_fall_degree_20261027/PREREGISTRATION.md`, and the recorded
  signed-Frobenius-orbit relation-rank census
  `research/rootfind_pair_collision_census_20261116/` (K13/K19/K23 cells).
- Wrote the instrument `examples/binary_falldeg_arms.rs`: three factor-base
  arms (orbit = squaring-closed subspace, random, polynomial-window), reuses
  the library builders and the `hky_par` closure (copied verbatim), emits
  `binary_falldeg/v1` JSONL with `d_ff` (rank proxy), `d_F` (HKY last fall),
  `d_res`, per-degree Macaulay/closure profiles, op/row counts, loadavg.
  Includes `#[test]`s (gate G1) and a `selftest` subcommand (G2/G4).
- Wrote `research/binary_falldeg_20261118/{PREREGISTRATION.md, README.md}`.
  Added the research dir + `pencil_large_prime_20261029` dep crate to the
  sparse checkout so cargo can parse the workspace.


- **Three arms** (orbit / random / subspace) with the orbit arm fixing
  `ℓ_eff`: a Frobenius(squaring)-invariant `F_2`-subspace has dimension = a
  sum of 2-cyclotomic-coset sizes, so it cannot be an arbitrary `ℓ`. The
  orbit arm builds to the nearest reachable dim; random + window match it.
- **`S_3` is `a_2`-independent** (read from source): Koblitz `a=0` and `a=1`
  give the identical summation system, so `a` is not a factor for `d_ff`/`d_F`.
  Recorded as a finding in the prereg, not a hypothesis.
- **`d_F` (HKY) is natively well-defined over GF(2^n)** (Weil descent lands
  in the Boolean ring; square-free normal form is free), unlike the prime
  field which needed a PKM tower. Computability (`~C(nv,≤c)` columns) is the
  binding limit, not definability.
- Reuse the tested library `first_fall_degree` (rank proxy) + `solving_degree`
  and the `hky_par` closure rather than writing a new engine (owner rule: no
  Python; reuse not rewrite).

### Pilot result (done)

Ran 72 rows (`raw/pilot.jsonl`), gates G1 (`4 passed`) and G4/ordering
(`selftest OK`). `d_F ∈ {2,3,4}`, flat, matches the prior binary program.
**No Frobenius-orbit-specific effect** on `d_F` vs random in range (every gap
≤ 1, at the A/A floor; where a structured base is lower, the non-Frobenius
polynomial window is lower too). The firmest finding is **H-avail**: a
`τ`-invariant orbit base of dimension `ℓ ≈ n/m` is dimension-constrained
(availability tracks `ord_n(2)`); unavailable at `n=13,19` for mid `ℓ`,
available at composite `n` and at `n=23` (`ℓ=11`). The library
`first_fall_degree` proxy exceeded `d_F` in several cells (rank proxy, not a
first-fall). `RESULTS.md`, `POD_PLAN.md`, `raw/dF_by_arm.svg` written.


- Pod ladder (`POD_PLAN.md`): `m=3, ℓ=7` to `cmax=5` at composite `n=21,25,27`
  to reach the first `d_F ≥ 5` and recheck the arms; needs owner price
  approval (not launched here).
- A proper PDF via `research-visuals` for the validated run (pilot left an SVG
  only; `[pcc:unverified]`).


- **Branch `wt/binary-falldeg` did not compile at HEAD `cfd51da7c0`**: a
  merge-conflict duplication in `src/cryptanalysis/f4_fp.rs` (the earlier fix

## 06:07 UTC — RR9 scope audit and shared-remainder lead selected (Codex)

### What was done

- Completed the bounded literature, lead-atlas, prior-experiment, red-team, protocol, and reference-method audits for PR #4675. The `research-kb` MCP was absent and every wrapper query (`status`, four mechanism/negative/application searches, and a theorem search) failed with `research-kb: no executable found`; fallback evidence came from the lead atlas, repository records, PKM PKC 2016, APS 2018, and McGuire--Mueller 2017/1262.
- Distinguished three non-equivalent mechanisms that had shared one label: the atlas `L(6O)` five-point norm/Graeffe lead, the older odd-arity `A,C,U` quotient/remainder tower, and the active `L(9O)` RR9 seven-coordinate predicate. An RR9 outcome cannot dispose of either predecessor without a proved reduction.
- Selected the exact RR9 candidate: for monic degree-eight `M(A,V,R)`, encode `H_B=Q_0M+R_0`, then `R_{j-1}^2=Q_jM+R_j` for three squaring layers and require `R_3=0`. A written derivation establishes a unique auxiliary witness and equivalence to `M | H_B^8`; the six factor-base rungs are `B={4,8,12,16,24,31}` with 44/52/56/60/68/75 variables and one more equation at each rung.
- Audited baselines. Cold one-target BSGS is usable only with full setup charged. The historical 1,024-target cached BSGS result remains a separate shared-advice diagnostic. Current ecbench online timing and phase-native accounting do not yet honestly split cached BSGS setup/search, and `rho.prime_fast` is not registered; versioned integration work is in progress.

### Decisions made

- Treat the six rungs as growth in factor-base size on one prime31 curve, not an ECDLP-exponent/T2 panel. B4/B8 are blinded complete-fibre correctness controls; B31 is the only whole-solve gate.
- The tower is multiplicity-aware. B4 starts from `H_4` and three remainder squarings, so it covers every degree-eight divisor of `H_4^8`; it is not restricted to the older `M=H_4^2` D9 control.
- A timeout, OOM, incomplete Groebner basis, unchecked UNSAT, missing target relation, or failed planted detector is inconclusive. Only exact oracle disagreement or a checked ideal certificate can disprove the corresponding frozen construction/stratum.
- No paid run starts until the prospective protocol, custody boundary, source, configuration, launch request, finite budget, and publication receipt are committed and pushed. The local host has no `cargo`, `rustc`, or Conductor.

### Open threads

- Finish and review the prospective protocol, machine-readable draft, search report, diagram/PDF, remainder-tower regression, native system generator, blinded grader boundary, and versioned baseline integration; then commit and push before any build.
- Compile and run deterministic controls on an authorized Rust environment. Only after B4/B8 complete-fibre gates pass may the bounded B31 whole solve and matched cold references begin.
- D9 one-dimensional-kernel extraction is a useful independent B8 positive control, but general multi-solution B4 fibres still require the tower/FGLM path or another complete solver.

## Single-target: gap to negation-only rho grows with bits; exponent argument (`[pcc:unverified]`)

### What was done
- Owner instruction: "single target only". Recomputed from the committed holdout rows (`results/pf_j0/sym4_holdout*`, 16 rows per cell, median in-row `rho_neg`): sym4 ms / rho_neg ms = 7.2/3.7 (28 bits, 1.9x), 15.3/4.4 (30, 3.5x), 35.9/6.5 (32, 5.5x), 196.1/22.3 (36, 8.8x), 534.0/31.0 (38, 17x). 3-summand: 4.3x, 7.5x, 14.9x, 47x, 98x. rho has about 2 ms fixed overhead and +-30% noise at 36-38 bits.
- Cost model for m summands: table about B^(m-1) entries (build cost B^(m-1)); about B relations needed, each costing p/B^(m-1) candidate scans, so scan cost p/B^(m-2). Balancing B^(m-1) = p/B^(m-2) gives B = p^(1/(2m-3)) and total cost p^((m-1)/(2m-3)) = p^(1/2 + 1/(2(2m-3))): m=3 -> 2/3, m=4 -> 3/5, m=5 -> 4/7, m=6 -> 5/9. The exponent stays strictly above 1/2 for every finite m (model; consistent with, not proven by, the measured growth of the gap).

### Decisions made
- Single-target sym-family cannot overtake rho (exponent 1/2) at any bit size by adding summands; m=5 would cut roughly 2x at 38 bits (model, unmeasured) against a 17x gap. Not implemented. Gains in this family are constant factors and a smaller gap, not parity. Reported as the distance remaining, not a claim.

### Open threads
- Different structure needed for parity (not a larger m); AWS-blocked gate artifacts; f4_fp.rs decision. Goal unmet.

### Errors
- None (an earlier draft of this entry had a garbled formula; corrected before pushing).

### What was done (commands, files, features)

Research KB MCP/wrapper unavailable in this connector runtime; direct GitHub primary sources and APS (2018), Bochum (2026) are the bounded fallback. No novelty claim from missing search matches.
- Created a fresh branch \`automation/prime31-rr9-weighted-resultant-20261118\`, recorded the task in ACTIVE, opened initial draft PR #4728, and committed the **prospective protocol before writing implementation** (\`RR9_WEIGHTED_SLOT_RESULTANT_PROTOCOL_20261118.md\`, first protocol commit \`6fd7fd6a7cab83e520e3acc5d74a5e9a2d9f813f\`).
- Derived exact slot-distinct necessary-and-sufficient supplied-chart criterion \`g_j=Res(H_j,M)=0\` with eight weight-specific public factor-base images; independent local Python BigInt preflight found 248/248 pairwise distinct weighted x's; 16/16 planted 8-slot signed charts recovered; 128/128 singleton polynomial slot roots and correct point replays; 16/16 same-target coefficient mutations rejected.
- Checked in new original-crate Rust example \`examples/prime31_rr9_weighted_resultants.rs\`, separate Python BigInt replay \`verify_prime31_rr9_weighted_resultants.py\`, source-specific GitHub Actions \`.github/workflows/prime31-rr9-weighted-resultants.yml\` with immutable Rust source SHA \`1df714645aa683cf975bc76fcacb9842cb4780fd\`, scientific report and ranked F4/incidence vs baseline experiment queue. First source-specific run https://github.com/isogeny-labs/crypto/actions/runs/44350433573 launched; its pending state must not be counted as PASS.

### Decisions made (and why)
- The shared-base \`H_B^8\` root-multiplicity filter and masked-slot positive support ceiling cannot establish high-yield weighted relation extraction. Distinct public scalar slot images let eight **separate** root constraints be checked algebraically without generic group half-sum tables.
- The exact criterion requires pairwise disjoint x sets; a duplicated-slot Hall counterexample shows false acceptance otherwise. The code checks the counterexample.
- Distinguish efficient certificate *evaluation* from public-target **coefficient recovery**. The factored 7-variable resultants have total polynomial degree <=62, so F4/incidence extraction remains an open problem, and no completed unknown-Q solver or frontier speedup is claimed.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)
